### Batch 136 — "for"-led headline continuations: MCO/FICO/KR/ROST period phrases joined; 24 stale AND-continuation rows repaired (2026-10-27)
Root cause in `data/filings.py`: some filers wrap the headline mid-phrase with the period phrase leading the second line — "MOODY'S CORPORATION REPORTS RESULTS" / "FOR THIRD QUARTER 2025", "FICO Announces Earnings of $7.40 per Share" / "for Third Quarter Fiscal 2025", "... Announces Guidance" / "for 2026", "ROSS STORES REPORTS RESULTS" / "FOR FOURTH QUARTER AND FISCAL 2024" — and batch 107's conjunction-lead rule only recognized and/or/but, so the period phrase was stored as the first body line and the headline read as if it had no period at all. Fix: `_HEADLINE_FOR_LEAD_RE` — a "for" lead is a trigger only with a period designator (ordinal quarter, or a bare year standing alone on its line). The narrowness is the point: a bare "for" lead is NOT a trigger, because lede "For ..." lines are common — but they always start "For the ..." ("For the three months ended ...:", "For the full year:") or are admin lines ("For 6:00 a.m. ET Release"), and the (?!the\b) guard plus the $-anchored bare year ("For 2026, the company expects ..." never matches) keep all three guard shapes out. **Live repair: 37 stored rows** — 13 FOR rows (MCO 3, FICO 8, KR 1, ROST 1) plus 24 stale AND-continuation rows (NKE, UNH, SPG, ARCC, HON, UPS, GIS, SJM, CVS, MPC) whose stored headlines predate the batch-107 AND rule and were never repaired: 23 by full live re-extraction (live body agrees with stored body up to paragraph grouping/bullet spacing and trailing extras), 14 by headline-splice (new headline + stored body byte-preserved) where the live body diverges for non-headline reasons — UNH Q2's live text drops the stored CEO-quote tail, UPS's live bullets re-space a "*", ARCC's table-section tail and MCO's section pick differ — so the splice fixes the headline without silently rewriting or losing body content. One row deliberately skipped: CART's "And price parity ..." is a genuine body sentence (live extraction None), not a continuation.

**Tests:** 5 new (MCO Q3 2025 end-to-end; FICO quarter-fiscal join; ROST quarter-and-fiscal join; KR bare-year join; guard negatives — Realty Income/Intuit colon labels, Lowe's admin line, "For the first quarter, revenue ..." lede, "For 2026, the company expects ..." outlook sentence). Full suite 1053 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** DB-wide scan over all 2425 stored highlight rows for for-led body-first lines found 37 (13 genuine continuations + 24 colon/admin "For the ..."/"For 6:00 ..." lines, all guarded out); old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 216 quarters (29 tickers incl. all cohort and guard companies) shows exactly 13 diffs, all FOR headline improvements, 0 elsewhere — and the AND-stale rows are identical under both code versions, confirming they needed data repair, not a code fix. Post-repair scan finds exactly 1 cohort row left (CART, by design). Live: /company/MCO, /FICO, /UNH, /NKE overview + detail/data 200, detail/data rendering joined headlines ("REPORTS RESULTS FOR THIRD QUARTER 2026", "for Third Quarter Fiscal 2026").

**Carried follow-ups:** STZ table-row wart (deliberate); CB partial residuals — Q3 2024 "Both Up Double-Digit…" wrap and Q4 2024 "Insurance" → "Up 15.7%…" wrap (with its dropped tail clause); ABNB heading-less metric table (shareholder-letter metric parse needs a highlights heading); CART "And price parity ..." sentence (genuine body text, not a continuation — no repair applies); ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence (stored bodies preserved by splice; the body drift itself is a separate extractor question); EOG honestly 8/8 blank (image-page narrative, structurally unextractable — no further extractor work will recover it short of OCR); SKYD 4 blank (structurally unextractable per batch 48) + PEG 2 + AFRM/CCI/LIN/PCAR 1 each; pending-filing cohort at 29 (re-verified live batch 132 — zero landings, genuinely still EDGAR lag, not stale notes); partial residuals incl. ### Batch 137 — Chubb "Income"/"Insurance" headline wraps closed: CB Q3 2024 "Both Up Double-Digit" tail and Q4 2024 dropped "Up 15.7%" tail clause repaired (2026-10-27)
Closes the batch-136 CB follow-ups. Root cause in `data/filings.py`: Chubb's paragraph headlines wrap at arbitrary points, and two wrap points had no trigger — Q3 2024 wrapped after "Net Investment Income" onto "Both Up Double-Digit; Global P&C and Life Insurance Net / Premiums Written were Up 8.5% and 10.6%, in Constant Dollars", so the tail clause sat in the body as two orphan paragraphs; Q4 2024 wrapped after "Life Insurance" onto "Up 15.7%; P&C Combined Ratio of 86.6%", and that tail clause dropped out of the stored text entirely (the old headline stopped at "Insurance" and the section extractor skipped the orphan line). Fix, in two halves with different evidence: "Insurance" joins the bare fragment-tail list (a complete headline never ends on it, and the blast shows zero over-joins across 264 quarters incl. 8 insurers); "Income" does NOT — the first blast run showed a bare "Income" tail over-joins genuine headlines (Carnival's "NET INCOME" headlines followed by subhead lines "Accelerates shareholder returns ..." / "Record bookings provide ...", PSEG's "$3.54 PER SHARE NET INCOME" followed by the sibling metric line "$3.68 PER SHARE NON-GAAP OPERATING EARNINGS") — so "Income" joins only conjunctively, with a "Both"-led continuation (`_HEADLINE_INCOME_TAIL_RE` + `_HEADLINE_BOTH_LEAD_RE`): "Both" is a pronoun referring back to the two income figures, never a subhead or a sibling metric line, and a sentence-terminated "Both ..." lede is still refused by the continuation guards. **Live repair: 2 stored rows, both by headline-splice** (new headline + stored body byte-preserved): Q3's live body content lines start with the preserved body (the join frees a paragraph slot, so live text carries 2 extra trailing bullets, deliberately not taken); Q4's marker-free live body is a prefix of the stored body (the new body_start leaks a "QUARTER"/"●" heading into the live body and its section pick stops after the first bullet block, while the stored body carries both blocks — so the splice keeps the fuller stored body and the leak stays a section-extractor follow-up, not laundered into the row).

**Tests:** 10 new (CB Q3 + Q4 end-to-end on the real exhibit lines; income+Both chain join; insurance+Up-figure join; guard negatives — sentence-terminated lede, bullet lede, fresh headline, Carnival subhead, PSEG sibling metric line, "Both" lead without an income tail). Post-repair scan: no stored headline ends on "Income"/"Insurance" with a stranded continuation left. ### Batch 138 — Lede-budget junk lines: Chubb "QUARTER"/"●" heading leak closed, 6 stored CB rows repaired (2026-10-27)
Closes the batch-137 CB Q4 follow-up — but the mechanism turned out to be the lede fallback, not the section extractor. Root cause in `data/filings.py`: Chubb's bullet lists flatten each marker onto its own line under a table-split section heading, so the live lede for Q4 2024 opened "QUARTER" / "●" / bullet 1 / "●" — and the 6-line lede cap stopped there, showing bullet 1 plus a dangling marker and cutting bullet 2 mid-item, while the stored row (older extraction) held both bullets complete. Fix, in the lede loop only: skip a lone all-caps word (a section-heading tail — "QUARTER" from "Fourth Quarter", "YEAR" from "Full Year" — never narrative prose) and skip a leading lone bullet marker (a marker before any collected line carries no content; the bullet's text follows). Mid-lede markers are kept: they are the only separators between bullets in the rendered paragraphs. **Live repair: 6 stored rows** (2024-06-30, 2025-03-31, 2025-06-30, 2025-09-30, 2025-12-31, 2026-03-31) — each gated on identical headline plus zero stored content paragraphs lost (live only drops junk and adds content); Q4 2024 needed no repair (fixed live extraction is byte-identical to its stored row, which is what proved the fix). The 2024-06-30 row sat outside the 8-quarter blast window and surfaced only in the post-repair scan — the scan, not the blast, closed the cohort. Post-repair scan: zero junk-leading CB rows (8/8 stored rows clean; 2026-06-30 has no stored row yet and will extract clean on next seed).

**Tests:** 5 new (CB Q4 2024 lede shape end-to-end — heading tail + leading marker out, both bullets complete, mid marker kept; mid-lede "YEAR" tail; fragment/marker guard negatives). Full suite 1068 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 360 quarters (46 tickers incl. all cohort, guard, insurer/bank companies) shows exactly 7 diffs, all CB, zero content lines lost anywhere (removed lines are only blanks, "QUARTER", "●"; added lines are bullet content or a repositioned mid marker). ### Batch 139 — Lede-budget separators: lone bullet markers never consume budget in marker-delimited ledes; CB Q3 2024 trailing bullets closed, 13 stored rows repaired (2026-10-27)
Closes the batch-138 CB Q3 follow-up — but the mechanism turned out to be the budget accounting, not the cap size. Root cause in `data/filings.py`: the lede fallback's 6-line budget counted every line, including lone bullet-marker separators — and Chubb's Q3 2024 markers ("·" middle dot, "○" white circle) were not even in `_is_lone_bullet_marker`, so the lead "·" was kept as a junk paragraph and the budget ran out after bullet 2 ("·" + 3 bullet-1 lines + "·" + bullet-2 line = 6), although the lede holds 8 bullets; the stored row (older extraction) held bullet 1 only. Fix, in the lede loop only: (1) "·" and "○" join the lone-marker predicate (lead markers of either kind are skipped, mid-lede ones kept as separator paragraphs); (2) markers never consume budget; (3) in a marker-delimited lede the current item is finished rather than cut mid-item — up to 4 continuation lines past the budget, and a marker at the boundary ends collection without being appended (no dangling marker). The finish rule is gated on a marker having been seen: a marker-less prose lede keeps the old hard 6-line cap, because without item delimiters "finish the item" is undefined — the ungated version changed 109/232 blast quarters (mostly prose extensions up to the 4000-char cap and table-row drift on ARCC) for no defect, and the gate is what rejected it. **Live repair: 13 stored rows** (CB 7 incl. Q3 2024, GIS 1, SPG 5) — each gated on identical headline plus every stored content paragraph present in the new body in order (the CB Q3 lead "·" paragraph is dropped by design, the only removed paragraph in the whole blast); CB 2026-06-30 has no stored row yet and will extract clean on next seed.

**Tests:** 6 new (CB Q3 2024 lede shape end-to-end — markers budget-free, bullets 1–4 complete, bullet 5 unreached; boundary marker never dangles; 4-line finishing cap; prose lede hard cap unchanged; lead "·"/"○" skipped; marker predicate incl. "·"/"○" with glued-text negatives). Full suite 1074 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new extraction over 232 quarters (29 tickers incl. all cohort, guard, insurer/bank companies) shows exactly 14 diffs after gating (the ungated run showed 109 — see above), all with identical headlines, 13 pure extensions ending on terminal punctuation and CB Q3's paragraph-shift (lead "·" out, bullets 3–4 in), zero content paragraphs lost anywhere. Live: /company/CB overview + detail + detail/data 200, detail/data for Q3 FY2024 rendering the repaired bullets 1–4; /company/SPG + /company/GIS overview 200.

### Batch 140 — Lone bullet markers are never stored: lede, section, and leading-block paths all drop them; predicate gains "▪"/"–"; 27 marker rows + 3 page rows remain gated-out (2026-10-27)
DB-wide audit of stored highlights found the junk class batch 138/139 left behind: 42 rows with a lone-marker paragraph (lede path — SPG/NUE/CB/CI/SNPS/SLB/GIS/KVUE/ON/NDAQ/VRSK/UNP), 50 rows with a lone-marker line inside a "\n"-joined paragraph (section path and leading-bullet-block path — Nucor's "•" per item, PNC's "▪", Simon's "–"), and 9 rows carrying "Page N of M" artifacts (4 bare MET paragraphs; UNH's inline "Page 1 of 16 <headline>" page-image mega-lines are a different shape and stay out of scope). Root cause in `data/filings.py`: batch 139 kept mid-lede markers as separator paragraphs on the theory that they separate bullets in the rendered output — but paragraphs are already blank-line separated, so a lone marker paragraph renders as a junk glyph line and adds no separation; the section collector and the leading bullet block never filtered markers at all. Fix, in all three paths: the lede loop no longer appends lone markers (marker_seen is still set, so the batch-139 finish rule and budget behavior are unchanged); the section collector and the leading bullet block drop lone-marker lines AFTER qualification, so their caps and floors see the same lines as before. The predicate gains the last two glyphs the audit found standing alone: "▪" (PNC) and "–" en dash (Simon — the hyphen-minus was covered, the en dash was not). This supersedes batch 138's "applying the skips to the section path or _leading_bullet_block" deliberately-not-done (that decision predated the audit evidence) and batch 139's keep-as-separator choice; 3 batch-139 expectations and 1 batch-138 expectation were updated to the new decision. **Live repair:** every row passing the gate was written (final round: SPG 2024-12-31, SPG 2025-12-31); the repair only ever removes junk paragraphs/lines, never imports live content — so SPG 2025-12-31's budget-freed FFO line (see blast note) is NOT in the stored row, same precedent as batch 137's untaken trailing bullets. Post-repair scan: 27 marker rows + 3 bare-Page rows remain, all accounted for — 14 outside the 8-quarter blast window (UNP, CB 2024 Q2, ELV, RMD 8, PEG, VRSK 2) plus gate-failed drift rows whose stored bodies diverge from live for non-marker reasons (PNC 7: stored bodies are table fragments the current extractor rejects; MET 3: page paragraph plus body drift; SPG 2024 Q3 + 2025 Q3, NUE 2024 Q4 + 2025 Q1, GIS 2025 Q2, KVUE 2025 Q1: headline or content drift). ### Batch 141 — Page markers glued to headlines are stripped; masthead-address headlines repaired: MET 3, EL 6, SPGI 8 (2026-10-28)
Closes the batch-140 UNH follow-up — but only the headline half. Root cause in `data/filings.py`: UnitedHealth's page-image exhibits flatten page 1 into one bullet-run line, and `_split_bullet_runs` leaves the page marker on the first piece ("Page 1 of 16 UnitedHealth Group Reports First Quarter 2026 Results") — the bare-page admin guard is `$`-anchored and never fires when headline text follows the marker, so the headline loop picked marker + headline verbatim. Fix, in the headline loop only: a new `_PAGE_PREFIX_RE` strips a leading "Page N of M" / "Page N" prefix and runs the remainder through the wrap joiner, and the strip is accepted only when the JOINED result is headline-shaped (a bare fragment remainder with no continuation keeps the old behavior — a 3-word fragment headline would be worse than a prefixed complete one). **Live repair: 17 stored rows** in two gated shapes, both preserving the stored body byte-for-byte: SHIFT (drop 1–3 junk masthead paragraphs — bare page marker, street address, city+ZIP, "News" kicker, company website — when the remaining stored lines equal a prefix of the live lines modulo spacing normalization for old-extraction spacing like "company ." / "30% 1") covers MET 3, EL 3, SPGI 8; SPLICE (headline replaced by the live headline when the stored body is byte-identical to the live body) covers EL 3 incl. the two rows whose bodies ended with a trailing "Page N of M" line, dropped with the splice. The first audit undercounted the cohort (it searched for "Page", which missed SPGI's pure-address headlines and the EL rows without page trailers); the post-repair headline-shape scan is what closed it. Post-repair scan: zero trailing page lines anywhere, and exactly 3 junk-headline rows left — all UNH, deferred by design (see below).

### Batch 142 — Page markers stripped from body lines; view-online trailers cut so the outlook bullet survives; UNH's 3 stored rows repaired (2026-10-28)
Closes the batch-141 UNH follow-up in full (both body defects, plus the repair). Root causes in `data/filings.py`, both UnitedHealth page-image flattening: (1) page 2+ markers glue onto body lines — a sentence continuation ("Page 2 of 16 business, with $400 million ..."), a bullet ("Page 2 of 17 • Optum supported ..."), or prose ("Page 2 of 21 The company has taken steps ..."); (2) the outlook bullet glues a "To view this information in a different format, including graphics, published on our website, click here: <url>" trailer (plus flattened following prose) onto itself, and `_CONTACT_LINE_RE`'s `www.` match skips the whole 1834-char line in the leading-bullet-block/section/lede paths — dropping the earnings outlook from all three UNH quarters. Fix, applied to body lines only, after headline selection (so batch 141's headline-loop guard is untouched): `_clean_body_line` strips a leading full-form "Page N of M" marker when a remainder exists and cuts a line at the view-online trailer when a prefix exists. Two deliberate boundaries: only the full "of M" form is stripped, so a prose sentence starting "Page 2 of the report ..." is never beheaded; and the glued prose after the trailer URL is dropped with the trailer — it was dropped with the whole line before, and keeping it would bloat the bullet item and push metric bullets past the 14-line block cap. **Live repair: UNH's 3 stored rows replaced with the post-fix live extraction** (Q1 2026, Q2 2026, Q4 2025) under gates (same-exhibit cached HTML, headline-shaped + prefix-free + URL-free live headline, outlook bullet present, no page prefix or trailer anywhere in the live text, stored row with a source; old-vs-new diff reviewed by hand). ### Batch 143 — Flattened-cell "Financial Highlights" tables no longer win the section; the lede serves instead; 17 stored rows repaired (2026-10-28)
Closes the batch-142 follow-up "table-header sections can still win when a release has no bullets at all". Root cause in `_looks_like_financial_table` (`data/filings.py`): the batch-97 guard only saw "(Dollars|USD|$" unit lines, "% change" wording, and $+% data rows on a single line — and three stored table shapes have none of those. FedEx's tables use units in words ("(In millions, except earnings per share)"), a "Percent Change" header in words, and period headers with split year cells; Simon's Q1 tables split every cell across lines ("$", "1,757,093", "$", "1,473,012", ...) with no % anywhere and no units line at all; AutoZone's "Store Count" table has no $ or % anywhere, just "Beginning stores 6,627 6,432" rows under a doubled "12 Weeks Ended" header. Fix, all on non-bullet lines (the batch-107 bullet guard is untouched): a line-start units parenthetical in words ("(In millions|thousands|billions") and a full-line "Percent Change" header are strong table signals; lone "$", bare numbers (including parenthesized negatives), period headers (including doubled "Three Months Ended Six Months Ended"), standalone "(Unaudited)"-style annotations, "Change"/"Percent" header fragments, and label + two-number count rows count as cells, with 3+ cells rejecting the section. The second pass (doubled period headers + header-only stubs) came from the blast itself: FedEx FY2026 Q2/Q3 first survived as six-line header-only sections, which the stub signals now reject. Two deliberate boundaries: the word-units check is anchored at line start so mid-sentence "(in millions)" in prose never fires, and the trailing-"Percent" cell check is case-sensitive so a wrapped narrative line ending in lowercase "percent" never counts; doubled date headers ("November 22, 2025 November 23, 2024") are month-guarded out of the count-row rule.

**Tests:** 11 new (FDX shape, SPG split-cell shape, AZO count shape, FY2026 header-only stub, doubled-date-header non-count, mid-sentence units non-table, single "(Unaudited)" line non-table, genuine narrative non-table, plus end-to-end FDX/SPG/AZO docs serving the lede with the table absent). ### Batch 146 — Flattened summary-table mode parses Airbnb's heading-less shareholder-letter table into bullets; 6 stored ABNB rows repaired (2026-10-28)
Closes the batch-143 follow-up "ABNB heading-less metric table". Root cause in `data/filings.py`: the batch-94 metric mode requires a "Highlights" heading line, which Airbnb's letter never has — its summary table ("Key Financial Measures" / "Key Business Metrics") sits at the document head, and text extraction flattens it into run-on lines (group-header words split around the first metric's value, "Revenue Key Financial $3.6B Measures"; wrapped labels split around their value, "Free Cash $1.3B Flow ("FCF")*"; Q4 letters carry quarter + full-year value columns), so the standard path served the flattened table as headline + prose. Fix: a third shareholder-letter mode, tried after the batch-94 metric mode and before the standard path, that parses the value-delimited token stream — label value [change …] — where a change phrase is "%" + "Y/Y" ["(ex-FX)"] or "%" + words ending in "margin"; a between-values piece splits into label continuation (before the first %, or up to the first parenthetical when there is no %), change phrases, and the next label; consecutive values merge into multi-column values, and a detected dual-column header ("Q4 2025; FY 2025") names the value order. Two implementation findings from the first failing runs: the table-start search must match the header fragment ("key financial"/"key business"), not the full phrase, because the split header never appears contiguously on one line; and a footnote star after a parenthetical belongs to the continuation, not the next label ("* Trailing Twelve Month" was the first-run symptom). Anything that does not fit the grammar fails the whole parse, so the old behavior is preserved — in particular the Q3/Q4 2024 letters, whose table cells interleave with narrative sentences, fail on the first prose change phrase ("10% compared to Q3 2023…") instead of emitting junk labels.

**Tests:** 5 new (Q2 2026 single-column shape incl. split headers and wrapped labels; Q4 2025 dual-column shape; Q1 2025 metric-without-changes shape splitting on the parenthetical; Q3 2024 prose-interleaved shape rejected; non-shareholder-letter gating). Full suite 1109 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 24 live quarters (ABNB 8 + DUOL/AFRM/COIN/CART 4 each) shows exactly 6 diffs, all ABNB shape-A quarters (Q1/Q2/Q3 2025, Q4 2025, Q1/Q2 2026) improving from ~1300-char flattened blobs to ~390-char bullet lists; ABNB Q3/Q4 2024 and all 16 other-filer quarters are byte-identical. **Live repair: 6 stored rows replaced** (ABNB Q1 2025–Q2 2026) under gates (stored blob holds a "Key Financial"/"Key Business" fragment; live re-extraction starts with "Shareholder Letter" and carries ≥5 bullets; every bullet value token appears verbatim in the stored blob — Q4 2025: 12 values verified). The probe/blast/repair scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch146_probe.py`, `batch146_probe2.py`, `batch146_blast.py`, `batch146_repair.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent. Live: /company/ABNB, /company/ABNB/detail, Q2 FY2026 + Q4 FY2025 detail/data, /company/DUOL, /company/COIN all 200; detail/data serves the repaired Q2 FY2026 bullets verbatim (Revenue $3.6B, Nights 148.3M) and the Q4 FY2025 dual-column header verbatim, with zero "Key Financial" residue.

Rechecked the pending-filing cohort (28 companies with a stored amber note, down from 29 — SPGI cleared on its own since batch 132) against live EDGAR companyfacts, using the same evidence the note logic itself uses — `_revenue_duration_facts`, revenue-chain duration facts only — so the batch-107 false-positive classes cannot read as landings. **Result: 6 landings, 22 still lagged, zero fetch failures.** BIIB, CB, CDNS, ENPH, PYPL, and WELL now carry revenue facts for the missing 2026-06-30 quarter-end; the other 22 (ABT, AEP, C, CARR, CLX, CTSH, DOW, DUK, ED, EIX, ETR, GD, HCA, HUM, PLD, PPL, SO, SRE, URI, V, VMC, VRSK) still have no revenue facts for their missing ends — genuinely still EDGAR lag three months after filing, not stale notes. ### Batch 148 — Bounded `_skip_toc` tail: a TOC followed directly by narrative no longer loses its headline; 0 live quarters change (2026-10-29)
Closes the standing `_skip_toc` over-consumption follow-up carried since batch 126. Root cause in `data/filings.py`: after the last page-number line, the skip consumed title-shaped lines without limit, so a combined packet whose narrative follows the TOC directly (short headline + short ledes, no Contacts: block, no >140-char line) lost its headline — demonstrated end-to-end, old code stored the first medium-length prose sentence ("Management highlighted broad-based strength in leasing and occupancy ...") as the headline. Fix: the tail after the last page number is bounded — when its first headline-shaped line appears (headline verb + results topic, no terminal sentence punctuation), the skip rolls back to that line, skipping any leading footnote marker with the tail (landing on "(1)" would trip the footnote-headline guard and lose the release); a tail with no headline-shaped line keeps the old return, so SPG's "(1)" + footnote + mini-TOC tail is byte-identical. Two honesty notes from the investigation: the lede fallback self-heals several overshoot shapes (headline=None + body from 0 re-reads the document and the TOC-fragment dropper cleans the entries), which is why the defect surfaced only in the medium-sentence shape; and the mini-TOC second loop in `_skip_toc` is dead code in all reachable paths (the main loop already stops only at a marker, a long line, or EOF) — left untouched as a minimal-diff choice, not a behavior to preserve.

**Tests:** 4 new (rollback to headline; rollback past footnote marker; SPG tail unchanged; end-to-end medium-sentence case). Full suite 1113 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 36 live quarters (SPG 8 + EOG 8 + ABNB/DUOL/AFRM/COIN/CART 4 each) shows exactly 0 diffs — SPG byte-identical, EOG still honestly blank, no false rollbacks on letter/standard filers; no stored-row repair applies, since no live quarter changes. The probe/blast scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch148_probe.py`, `batch148_blast.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent. Live: /company/SPG, /company/SPG/detail, /company/EOG, /company/ABNB all 200.

### Batch 151 — Contact-block lookahead skips admin lines: Crown Castle's contact-detail line no longer becomes the headline; CCI Q1 FY2026 stored row repaired (2026-10-29)
Root cause in `data/filings.py` `_skip_contact_block`: the batch-105 lookahead treats a content-shaped name/title line as contact detail only when an email/phone follows within the next two lines. ### Batch 153 — Lede/section table skip: outlook tables no longer fill the highlights; 11 stored rows repaired (2026-10-30)
Closes the batch-149/150/151/152 follow-up "CCI lede tail-trim rule candidate" and the "CCI bullet-vs-lede gap" — and the APP guidance-heading tail from batch 152. Root cause, found by probing the live exhibits rather than the stored rows: Crown Castle opens its releases with the outlook table directly after the dateline paragraph, so the lede loop filled its 6-line budget with the table itself (unit line, outlook-table headings, header lines, data rows) and the real highlights bullets after the table starved. The stored rows were the better half of the defect (real bullet + 2-line wart tail); the live extraction was worse (table headers as the whole body). The fix skips table lines in the lede loop — they neither consume budget nor get stored — so the prose after the table is collected instead; a trailing table run is trimmed after collection; and the leading-bullet block and the Highlights-section collector both end at a table heading instead of swallowing it (AppLovin's "First Quarter 2026 Financial Guidance Summary" heading after the bullets).

**Predicates (the hard part was wrapped prose):** `_html_to_text` keeps literal newlines inside block elements, so a long paragraph arrives as unterminated mid-sentence fragments — "fiscal 2026 second quarter results with total revenue of $16.0 billion, up 8% in" looks exactly like an unterminated $-carrying line, and the first version of the skip ate Oracle/Chubb/USB wrapped fragments (5 test failures). The final predicates are strict about that: data rows must be figure-dense (2+ figures, ≥40% of tokens; a standalone "%" counts so spaced-out "67 %" rows qualify; the figure regex never matches a lone comma, which had made Chubb bullet fragments score as figure-dense); outlook/guidance headings must be pure header vocabulary (a prose clause mentioning the outlook fails on its verbs); period/date headers must be all period/date tokens with a year or month present; labels must be short and never $-carrying ("$50.2 billion" is a metric value, kept); terminated lines and bullets are never table lines. A trailing bare highlights heading ("Additional Financial Highlights:" whose bullets never arrived) is trimmed the same way.

**Tests:** 5 new (CCI-shaped lede skip incl. wordy data-row label, 9-word period header, and all-caps section heading; APP guidance-summary tail in the lede and in the section and leading-block paths; direct predicate checks incl. spaced-% rows, date headers, and the wrapped-prose negatives). Full suite 1122 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 36 quarters (CCI/SPG/EOG/ABNB/DUOL/COIN/CART/AFRM/APP, 4 each; HTML cached so both runs see byte-identical input) shows exactly 9 diffs, all intended: CCI Q3/Q4 2025 + Q2 2026 table-header soup → clean bullets (Q3/Q4 2025 now serve the single-bullet Highlights section the old code misclassified as a table and rejected, so the richer lede quote no longer serves — the section path is the designed primary, and the section is correct); APP Q3/Q4 2025 + Q1/Q2 2026 guidance tails gone (Q3 2025 gains its third bullet, the buyback-authorization one); COIN Q4 2025 salutation lines gone and more letter prose arrives; CART Q2 2026 outlook-heading tail gone. The other 27 quarters are byte-identical, including all shareholder-letter and image-only filers. **Live repair: 11 stored rows replaced** under gates (live source label equals stored; every substantive stored line survives verbatim modulo bullet-marker spacing, checked on its first 100 chars to tolerate old-code truncation; live carries no table-residue markers; live ≥200 chars): CCI Q4 FY2024 (773 → 1646, real headline replaces the stored date-line headline, bullets + quote), Q1 FY2025 (922 → 935), Q2 FY2025 (771 → 786), Q3 FY2025 (807 → 782), Q4 FY2025 (957 → 788), Q2 FY2026 (1309 → 1069); APP Q3 FY2025 (800 → 685, table dropped, 3 bullets), Q4 FY2025 (606 → 559), Q1 FY2026 (836 → 528), Q2 FY2026 (966 → 538); COIN Q4 FY2025 (402 → 637, salutation + date line gone, more letter prose). Deliberately NOT repaired: CART Q2 FY2026 — live picks the business-highlights section while stored holds the financial bullets (GTV/orders/revenue), so replacing it would destroy good content; only its 1-line "Three Months Ended June 30," tail wart remains, as a follow-up. The probe/blast/repair scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch153_probe.py`, `batch153_blast.py`, `batch153_repair.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent. Live: /company/CCI, /company/CCI/detail, /company/CCI/detail/data, /company/APP, /company/APP/detail, /company/COIN, /company/COIN/detail, /company/CART/detail all 200 (cold-refresh renders 0.25–123s); detail/data serves zero "Outlook Midpoint" / "Financial Guidance Summary" residue, and the repaired rows stayed repaired after page visits.

**Carried follow-ups:** Still open: ABNB Q3/Q4 2024's prose-interleaved tables (deliberately unparsed); CART "And price parity …" sentence (genuine body text — no repair applies) + CART Q2 FY2026's stored "Three Months Ended June 30," tail (period-header orphan; live picks a different section, so a wholesale replace would regress — needs a targeted trim, not a re-extract); ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence (stored bodies preserved by splice; the body drift itself is a separate extractor question); AFRM Q4 FY2026 + LIN Q3 2024 image-only blanks (OCR-only) alongside EOG 8/8 honest blanks and SKYD 4 pre-merger blanks; CCI's long table-label lines ("Organic Contribution to Site Rental Billings as Adjusted for Impact of Sprint Cancellations and DISH Terminations(b)", 12 words — the footnote-tail rule stops at 6 words, and a noun-phrase test is needed before extending it, since a prose fragment can end in a footnote marker too); APP Q3 FY2025's "Quarter Ended September 30, Nine Months Ended September 30," period header is now skipped by the date-header rule, but the same shape inside a section is only cut when a unit/heading line follows it; APP Q4 FY2024's stored all-table row (636 chars, headline + quarter/year table, no bullets) is a separate all-table class, not a tail wart; STZ live quote paragraphs interleave two-column quote text (real content, flattening artifact — the bullets are clean); pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s (new seeds' descriptions blank — external bot-block, not a pipeline defect); the batch-121 transient-window limitation (9M restated before the next 10-K restates the annual) still stands.

### Batch 154 — Space-less bullets are bullets: the bullet regex no longer requires a space, so Highlights sections stop being rejected as tables; 5 stored rows repaired (2026-10-30)
Closes the CCI bullet-vs-lede gap for the last affected quarter (Q3 2024) and the CART Q2 FY2026 tail wart from batch 153. Root cause, found by tracing why the live extraction for CCI Q3 2024 returned the lede instead of the 7-bullet "HIGHLIGHTS FROM THE QUARTER" section: `_BULLET_LEAD_RE` required a whitespace after the bullet glyph, but flattened exhibits emit "•Site rental revenues." with the text glued to the glyph — a DB sweep finds space-less bullet lines in 1118 of 2439 stored bodies across ~180 tickers, so this is the dominant bullet shape, not a CCI quirk. The missed guard defeated the batch-107 bullet guard in `_looks_like_financial_table`: 3 space-less $+%-carrying CCI bullets plus the $+%-carrying CFO quote tripped the data_rows >= 4 threshold, the genuine section was rejected as a financial table, and the lede fallback served the outlook table instead. The same failure discarded Instacart's financial Highlights sections, so the business section won and the batch-102 financial priority never fired for CART. The fix lets the glyph bullets (•/●/–/—) stand without a following space; the hyphen stays strict (space required) so a "-$1,234" negative table value still counts as a data row. The same regex guards all five use sites (table check, table-cell check, lede table-line, lede tail continuation). A second change, surfaced by the fix: the Highlights-section collector now also breaks on date headers ("Three Months Ended June 30,") — the batch-153 deferred gap — because CART's newly served financial sections otherwise ended on that orphan line.

**Tests:** 4 new, all fail without the fix (verified by stashing the src change): CCI-shaped end-to-end (space-less bullets + tables + quote → section wins, all 4 bullets serve, no "Change % Change" residue); direct `_looks_like_financial_table` checks (space-less and spaced bullets guarded, hyphen-led negative rows still read as a table); `_is_lede_table_line` predicate (space-less figure-dense bullet kept, hyphen-led row still a table row); section ends at a date header (CART-shaped, financial section wins over the business one). ### Batch 155 — Mixed-case datelines break the leading-bullet block; "(unaudited)"-suffixed Highlights headings leak table scaffolding; 8 GOOGL rows repaired (2026-10-30)
Closes the GOOGL table-fragment cohort carried since batch 154 — but the batch-154 premise was wrong in one respect, and the repair gates caught it: live extraction was NOT already clean for all 8 quarters. The 6 non-Q4 quarters were clean (lede shape: dateline + 5 bullets), but both Q4 quarters returned a 310-char table-intro stub ("The following table summarizes..." + the "Quarter Ended December 31, Year Ended December 31," period header), strictly worse than the stored 649/652-char table fragments. Root cause, found by tracing the Q4 exhibit through the extractor's pieces: `_DATELINE_LEAD_RE` required all-caps location segments, but Alphabet's dateline is "MOUNTAIN VIEW, Calif. – February 4, 2025 – ..." — the mixed-case state ("Calif.") never matched, so `_leading_bullet_block` broke at the dateline (first body line, not a bullet) and returned None. Q1–Q3 hid the failure because the lede fallback still served dateline + bullets; Q4 exposed it because the "Q4 2024 Financial Highlights" table section was the only section candidate and won by the earliest-candidate rule. The fix allows mixed case in both location segments of `_DATELINE_LEAD_RE` (the comma + capital-led state + spaced-dash shape still keeps prose out: no digits in the class, so a "Q4"-led line never matches, and a lowercase-led second segment never matches). A second change, surfaced by the blast: `_HIGHLIGHTS_HEADING_RE` now allows a trailing parenthetical after the closing word ("Q1 2025 Financial Highlights (unaudited)") — Alphabet suffixes Q1/Q2 2025's headings that way, and without it the leading block swallowed the heading plus the table-intro sentence as a bullet continuation (the first blast showed the 2-line wart on both quarters). The batch-97 guard is preserved: "Highlights" must still head or close the line, so a mid-sentence "highlight" verb in footnotes still never matches.

**Tests:** 7 new, 5 fail without the fix (verified by stashing the src change; the 2 boundary negatives pass either way, as designed): mixed-case dateline unit test (block found, dateline excluded); lowercase-state and digit-led negatives; GOOGL Q4-shaped end-to-end (bullets + quote win, no table intro/period header/data rows); GOOGL Q3-shaped end-to-end (bullets + quote, dateline dropped); heading-regex unit test (suffixed heading matches, footnote verb still doesn't); GOOGL Q1-shaped end-to-end with the "(unaudited)" heading (no heading/intro tail). Full suite 1133 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 62 quarters across 14 filers (the batch-154 cohorts + all 8 GOOGL quarters; HTML cached so both runs see byte-identical input) shows exactly 8 diffs, all GOOGL, all intended: Q4 FY2024/FY2025 table-intro stub → 5 bullets + CEO quote; the other 6 quarters lede (dateline + bullets) → leading block (bullets + quote, dateline dropped, dividend bullet gained where present). The other 54 quarters are byte-identical, including all shareholder-letter and image-only filers. **Live repair: 8 stored rows replaced** under gates (re-fetched inside the repair script; live source label equals stored; live ≥200 chars; live headline equals stored headline; live carries no table-fragment markers; live carries bullets; every stored bullet core survives in the live body; live ≠ stored): Q3 FY2024 (513 → 965), Q4 FY2024 (649 → 1554), Q1 FY2025 (1026 → 1672), Q2 FY2025 (1184 → 1819), Q3 FY2025 (1256 → 1511), Q4 FY2025 (652 → 1524), Q1 FY2026 (1080 → 1872), Q2 FY2026 (1070 → 1146). The repair ran in two passes: the first (pre-fix) pass repaired 6 rows to the lede shape and correctly skipped the 2 Q4 rows on the residue gate; the script was then extended to accept both stale shapes (table fragment or dateline-led lede without the quote) and re-ran to the final shape. Post-write DB read confirms the fragment sweep is 0 rows and all 8 rows carry the CEO quote; the 9th GOOGL row (Q3 FY2026, 1145 chars) was already in the new shape. The probe/blast/repair scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch155_probe.py`, `batch155_liveprobe.py`, `batch155_q4probe.py`, `batch155_q4dump.py`, `batch155_q4trace.py`, `batch155_q4trace2.py`, `batch155_blast.py`, `batch155_repair.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent. Live: home, /company/GOOGL, /company/GOOGL/detail, /company/GOOGL/detail/data, /company/CCI, /company/CART, /company/WELL all 200 (7 checks post-restart); detail and detail/data serve the repaired highlights with "Sundar Pichai" present and zero "following table summarizes" residue. The dev server was restarted via the documented launcher (kill by PID, never pkill) to load the new code; a fresh server process came up on the restart (PIDs 2946/2947).

**Carried follow-ups:** Still open: ABNB Q3/Q4 2024's prose-interleaved tables (deliberately unparsed); CART "And price parity …" sentence (genuine body text — no repair applies); ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence (stored bodies preserved by splice; the body drift itself is a separate extractor question); AFRM Q4 FY2026 + LIN Q3 2024 image-only blanks (OCR-only) alongside EOG 8/8 honest blanks and SKYD 4 pre-merger blanks; the footnote-tail rule still stops at 6 words (CCI's long labels never reach extracted output; the GOOGL fragment that motivated it is now closed); WELL Q3 FY2026's dangling "Recent Investment Activity" subheading tail (the section collector's 12-line cap cuts mid-subsection; a title-case subheading break or tail trim is a separate heuristic); GOOGL multi-line CEO quotes truncate to the first terminated line in the leading block (Q2 FY2026's quote ends at its first sentence — the block's item-close rule; a finish-the-quote rule is a separate heuristic); a table-headed "Financial Highlights" section with no bullets still wins as the only candidate and pre-empts the lede when no leading block exists (the Q4 hazard, now masked for GOOGL by the dateline fix, but the section-path rule itself is unchanged — a no-bullet table-section rejection is a separate fix); APP Q4 FY2024's stored all-table row (636 chars, headline + quarter/year table, no bullets) is a separate all-table class, not a tail wart; STZ live quote paragraphs interleave two-column quote text (real content, flattening artifact — the bullets are clean); pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s (new seeds' descriptions blank — external bot-block, not a pipeline defect); the batch-121 transient-window limitation (9M restated before the next 10-K restates the annual) still stands.

### Batch 156 — No-bullet table-headed "Highlights" sections no longer pre-empt the lede; lede skips table residue after a rejected section (2026-10-30)
Closes the batch-155 follow-up: the section-path rule itself is now fixed, not just masked for GOOGL. In `_extract_highlights_sections`, a section that hits a table break with no bullet lines is skipped (tried against later headings) even when its collected intro prose runs past the old 150-char thin-section floor — the GOOGL Q4 shape ("The following table summarizes..." + "Quarter Ended December 31, Year Ended December 31,", which `_looks_like_financial_table` never flags) used to win as the only candidate and serve a 314-char table stub instead of the lede's real bullets. "·"-led bullets count as bullets (batch 138/139), so a "·"-bulleted section is never rejected for lacking a "•"-style lead. Four lede-residue skips, needed because rejecting the section alone left the lede starved by the section's residue (the first fix pass improved the synthetic only 314 → 681 chars with zero bullets): the lede loop now skips "Highlights" section headings; `_is_lede_table_line` treats a "following table / table below summarizes" sentence as table residue; `_LEDE_DATE_TOKEN_RE` gains full/year/years so a doubled period header ("Quarter Ended December 31, Year Ended December 31,") is a date header; `_LEDE_DATA_ROW_RE` allows a space-separated $ ("Revenues $ 86,310 $ 96,469"), which flattened tables write everywhere. The blast caught one overreach in the first pass: "sets forth" was in the intro-verb list, but Crown Castle Q1 2026's genuine lede sentence ("The following table sets forth Crown Castle's current full year 2026 Outlook, which includes a $40 million decrease...") matched it, so the lede lost a real sentence and gained a tagline plus an "Amortization of prepaid rent 65 to 95 —" table row — the verb list is now "summarizes" only (attested in Alphabet/Adobe exhibits), with a test pinning the CCI sentence as prose.

### Batch 157 — Rule lines + trailing table stubs dropped; $-less data rows recognized; 35 stale stored rows repaired (2026-10-30)
Closes the batch-156 follow-up: the gated repair pass over the stale-stored-highlights cohort (ADBE/META/MTB/APP/MCO/EMR). Three extractor fixes, all surfaced by the repair gates themselves. (1) `_clean_highlights_result`, a final cleanup on the assembled body: horizontal-rule lines are dropped anywhere (Meta's 36-underscore rule between the bullets and the footnote), and a trailing table stub is trimmed — a lone table-intro sentence (also when it ends with a colon, Adobe's "targets:" shape) and/or a heading-shaped line (`_is_trailing_heading`: ≤8 words, every word capitalized/number/small-word, ending with a section word — Highlights/Results/Commentary/Targets/Summary/Overview/Update/Guidance/Outlook/Performance/Review/Measures/Financials/Earnings/Activity/Discussion — never bullet- or dash-led, no $/%). The headline is never trimmed (only lines after the first paragraph break), and the trim is kept only when the result stays ≥200 chars (the batch-153 floor rule). The end-word rule exists because the first version (no end-word rule) ate a truncated headline fragment — "Goldman Sachs Reports Second Quarter Earnings Per Common" is heading-shaped (7 capitalized words, unterminated) — plus the banner below it, dropping a 248-char Goldman cover result under the 200-char floor; a test pins the fragment as a negative. (2) `_is_lede_data_row` now recognizes $-less figure-dense rows (the density rule is the guard) and label + 2-$ -figure short lines ("Restructuring / Related Costs ~$0.06 ~$0.20", 2 figures in 6 tokens = 33%), with a terminated-prose guard protecting direct callers — but only for short lines (≤80 chars): the first blast showed the un-capped version swapping Tesla's whole FINANCIAL SUMMARY mega-line for its OPERATIONAL SUMMARY mega-line (both are single figure-dense lines), and the length cap restores the old behavior for mega-lines. (3) The trailing-intro regex accepts ":" endings (Adobe's targets intro).

**Tests:** 8 new, all in `tests/data/test_filings_batch157.py` (rule-line + dangling-heading end-to-end, trailing heading+intro pair, heading predicate incl. the Goldman fragment negative, headline-never-trimmed, multi-sentence intro-line kept, dash rule lines, $-less data rows incl. the mega-line negative, colon-ending intro trim). 2 end-to-end tests fail with the call-site disabled, so the regression is pinned. ### Batch 158 — Multi-line CEO quotes finish in the leading-bullet block; closing-quote terminal check drops JNJ table-heading residue; 8 stale stored rows repaired (2026-10-30)
Closes the multi-line-quote follow-up carried since batch 155. In `_leading_bullet_block`, the item-close rule broke the block at the first line ending in terminal punctuation — but a multi-line CEO quote's opener ends mid-quote ("Sundar Pichai, CEO of Google and Alphabet, said: “Our AI investments are redefining what's possible across every part of our business."), so GOOGL Q2 FY2026's stored highlights ended at that sentence and the remaining three quote lines never served. Two helpers now judge the close: `_item_has_open_quote` scans the current item's quote state (curly quotes nest by depth, a straight `"` toggles, mixed forms compose), and `_current_leading_item_text` scopes that state to the current item so an earlier item's closed quote can't keep a later item open. While the quote is open the break is skipped; the 14-line cap and the heading/boilerplate breaks above still bound the block, so an unclosed quote cannot run away. A second change in the same `if`: the terminal-punctuation regex now sees through a closing quote (`.”` / `."` count as terminal). The old regex never matched a line ending in `”`, so a quote that opened and closed on one line never closed the item itself — the next prose line leaked in before the rule fired. That leak is what put JNJ's "Overall financial results" / "Q4" table headings into stored bodies: JNJ's dateline paragraph is one mega-line ending `...technology.”`, and the old code appended the table's header lines as continuations before the table-units break stopped it.

**Tests:** 7 new in `tests/data/test_filings_batch158.py` (quote-state unit tests incl. straight/mixed forms and the current-item scoping; multi-line-quote block test on the GOOGL Q2 FY2026 shape; closed-quote-still-closes boundary; straight-quote multi-line boundary; unclosed-quote bounded-by-heading; GOOGL Q2-shaped end-to-end). The regression is pinned by stashing the src change: the old code truncates the shaped block to 3 lines ending mid-quote with no closing quote, and the new tests fail to import (the helpers don't exist). Full suite 1155 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 117 quarters across 22 filers (the batch-157 cohort; HTML cached so both runs see byte-identical input) shows exactly 8 diffs, all intended: GOOGL 5 (Q3 FY2024, Q4 FY2025, Q1/Q2/Q3 FY2026 — each quote now completes through its closing `”`, e.g. Q2 gains the Gemini-token / World-Cup lines and ends "globally.”"), JNJ 3 (Q4 FY2025, Q1/Q2 FY2026 — the "Overall financial results" + quarter table-heading lines drop out; the dateline paragraph itself is unchanged). The other 109 quarters are byte-identical, including all shareholder-letter, image-only, and Tesla quarters. **Live repair: 8 stored rows replaced** under the batch-157 gates (re-fetched inside the repair script; live source label equals stored; live ≥200 chars; headline match; every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence survives; live carries no table residue — the removed JNJ lines are unterminated headings under the 30-char narrative floor, so they pass the gate by design): GOOGL Q3 FY2024 (965 → 1547), Q4 FY2025 (1524 → 2209), Q1 FY2026 (1872 → 2222), Q2 FY2026 (1146 → 2148), Q3 FY2026 (1145 → 1994); JNJ Q4 FY2025 (1868 → 1832), Q1 FY2026 (1774 → 1675), Q2 FY2026 (1787 → 1752). ### Batch 162 — Bare numbered footnotes ("1) See Table A ...") no longer leak into the lede or a Highlights section; 8 stale stored rows repaired (2026-10-31)
Closes the batch-161 follow-up (numbered-footnote lines). A DB-wide scan finds 4 stored rows carrying one: SRE 2024-09-30 / 2025-03-31 / 2025-06-30 ("1) See Table A for information regarding non-GAAP financial measures.") and DDOG 2025-06-30 ("1) The three months ended June 30, 2025 are adjusted for M&A transaction costs of $1.4 million, ..."). Neither matches the parenthesized footnote rule ("(1) ...", batch 97) or the definition-verb rule ("1 Defined as ...", batch 104), so the line is collected as lede prose — in SRE it sits mid-lede between the "following table" paragraph and the next real paragraph, consuming a budget slot. New `_LEDE_FOOTNOTE_NUM_RE` (`^\d{1,3}\)\s`) in `data/filings.py`, plugged into three places: the lede-loop skip (with the other two footnote rules), `_is_lede_table_line` before the termination gate (batch-156 precedent — a terminated sentence can still be table residue, so the tail trim can start on it), and the headline skip (the batch-104 hazard in its bare form: a footnote definition ahead of the headline is never the headline). The first blast (3 diffs, SRE only) caught a second gap on review: DDOG is section-served, not lede-served — its footnote sits between section bullets — and the section collector is the one collection path with no footnote handling at all. So new `_is_section_footnote_line` (all three footnote forms: parenthesized, definition-verb, bare numbered) drops footnote lines from a collected section AFTER the 150-char qualification, following the batch-140 lone-marker pattern so the floor and the 12-line cap see the same lines as before.

### Batch 164 — Copula-pointer footnote definitions are never headline or lede material; SKYD's footnote headline fixed (2026-10-31)
Closes the batch-163 follow-up in full. Skydance's Q2 FY2026 shareholder letter (`ex99_q226.htm`) opens with "1 Non-GAAP measures are detailed in the Supplemental Disclosures at the end of this letter." — no closing paren, so the batch-162 bare-numbered rule (`^\d{1,3}\)\s`) never saw it, and no definition verb, so the batch-104 rule never saw it either; the headline loop picked it as the headline, and the same line trailed the Q1 FY2026 lede. **fix** (`data/filings.py`): `_FOOTNOTE_DEF_RE` gains a third alternative — numeric key + defined term + copula + definition pointer (`(are|is)\s+(detailed|defined|described|calculated|reconciled|set forth)`). The participle list is deliberately definition-pointer only: "are included / provided / presented / discussed" stay OUT, because a wrapped bullet continuation can legitimately read "1 million shares are included in ..." — those verbs are prose, not footnote pointers. The alternative carries a 300-char line bound (`^(?=.{0,300}$)`), which is load-bearing: the copula may sit anywhere in the line, so an unbounded match swallowed SHOP Q3 2024's page-1 mega-line whole (it opens "1 Internet, Everywhere ..." and contains "are reconciled ..." a thousand chars in), dropping the entire page from the lede, killing the batch-163 salvage, and serving the outlook + balance sheet instead — the blast caught it, the bound fixed it, and a mega-line non-match test pins it. Because the guard lives in the shared regex, the headline loop, lede filter, section filter, and `_is_section_footnote_line` all gain the shape at once. Same batch also closes the other batch-163 follow-up: `_leading_bullet_block` now skips a footnote-definition line instead of swallowing it as a bullet continuation (no attested case there; the section filter already dropped these lines). **Live repair: 2 stored rows replaced** under the batch-157 gates (re-fetched inside the repair script; live source label equals stored; live ≥200 chars; every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence survives; live carries no table residue), with the headline gate branched on the stored shape — Q2's stored headline IS the defect, so live must be the first stored bullet and match no footnote regex; Q1's stored headline is real narrative, so live must keep it verbatim and the footnote tail must be gone: SKYD 2026-06-30 (580 → 637, footnote headline out, first bullet becomes the headline, two more bullet lines served), SKYD 2026-03-31 (2083 → 2693, footnote tail out, letter-opening paragraph gained). ### Batch 165 — Lede finish rule gains a wrap-tail trigger; SKYD's mid-bullet cut and EMR's mid-quote cut repaired (2026-11-01)
Closes the batch-164 follow-up in full — with a corrected attribution: the cut is the LEDE's 6-line budget, not the section/leading-block budget. Skydance's "Summary Points:" heading sits before the headline (bullet 1 becomes the headline), so no Highlights section is ever found and `_leading_bullet_block` sees a continuation line first and returns None; the lede fallback serves the list, and the batch-139 finish rule never fires because inline-marked bullets never set `marker_seen`. **fix** (`data/filings.py`): the finish rule gains a second trigger, `_LEDE_WRAP_TAIL_RE` — a lede line that ends grammatically unfinished (trailing comma/dash, or a trailing conjunction/preposition/article/copula, including the attested "across"). The decision is snapshotted at the first line past the budget (a continuation line's own ending is an arbitrary wrap point, not evidence the item finished), and for the wrap-tail path collection stops once a continuation line terminates the sentence (`_is_sentence_terminated`) — the unfinished item is a sentence, so the next line starts a new item. A new bullet starting past the budget ends collection at the boundary (`_is_inline_bullet_start`), so no bullet is ever started that cannot finish. A complete statement does NOT trigger the rule, even without terminal punctuation: a figure bullet ("•Share repurchases and dividends of $5.1B", "•Diluted EPS was $0.78") and a complete clause ("...up 181% and 148% year over year, respectively") end the item. **Blast-radius story:** the first version generalized batch 139's gate by setting `marker_seen` on inline bullet starts — the blast over 268 quarters showed 34 diffs, of which only SKYD was intended: 22 prose extensions (AON/DUK/DELL CEO-quote paragraphs, one pulling in a "Duke Energy News Release 2" page footer), V/NKE/DELL figure-bullet extensions, and a COIN footnote-definition pull. That version was discarded; the wrap-tail gate re-blasted to 3 diffs (SKYD, EMR, COIN — the COIN one fired on a trailing semicolon), and dropping ";" from the wrap tail (a semicolon separates clauses; Coinbase's letter ends a paragraph with one right before its footnote block) left exactly 2 diffs, both intended. **Live repair: 2 stored rows replaced** under the batch-157 gates (re-fetched inside the repair script; live source label equals stored; live ≥200 chars; every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence survives; live carries no table residue; both stored headlines real narrative, kept verbatim): SKYD 2026-06-30 (637 → 746, bullet 3 completes "Television Studios, Paramount Animation, and Skydance Sports for both first- and third-party distribution."), EMR 2024-09-30 (634 → 1159, the CEO quote finishes "...continued momentum in fiscal 2025."). The probe/diagnose/blast/classify/repair scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch165_probe.py`, `batch165_diagnose.py`, `batch165_diagnose2.py`, `batch165_blast.py`, `batch165_classify.py`, `batch165_repair.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent.

### Batch 166 — Footnote-definition blocks skip as blocks; 4 stale EMR rows repaired (2026-11-01)
Closes the batch-165 follow-up in full — the multi-line footnote skipper is now the single gate for the whole hazard class, at all four collection paths (headline loop, lede, leading-bullet block, Highlights sections) plus the salvage path's sentence check. **fix** (`data/filings.py`): `_footnote_def_block_len(lines, i)` returns how many lines the footnote-definition block starting at `lines[i]` occupies, or 0. Openers: the three single-line shapes, the bracketed shape (`[1] Adjusted EBITDA is a non-GAAP financial measure.` — Coinbase's Q2 FY2025 letter served its [1]/[2] footnotes as the lede), the fourth shape (numeric key + bare capitalized term, definition verb on the next line: `1 Coinbase Total Trading Volume / comprises Spot Trading Volume`), and the same-line term+verb variant (`3 Trading Volume represents the`, `4 Crypto Asset Volatility represents`, `1 Spot Trading Volume is` — the last one is why shape 2 misses it: its participle is "presented", outside shape 2's list; a bare `is`/`are` counts only at line end or before a definition participle, so `1 Total Revenue is expected to grow` never opens). The term's first word must be Title-Case but later words may be lowercase (Emerson's `2 Underlying orders do not include AspenTech.`, `2 Underlying sales exclude/excludes the impact ...`), with an optional `do not / does not` before the verb. Consumption: only sentence termination ends a definition — there is deliberately no completeness gate (a wrapped line can look complete: `[3] $USD resources is defined as cash and cash` carries a 5-word object yet wraps onto `equivalents and USDC ...`); an unterminated line consumes its next line only when it is a plausible wrap (lowercase-led, definition-verb-led, conjunction-led, a `U.S.`-style abbreviation lead, a `US`-style all-caps lead, a `$`/digit-led figure line, or a bare-term opener — a year-led sentence `2025 was a strong year ...` is never a wrap). After a definition terminates, the block chains only onto another definition opener. Hard boundaries are structural only (bullets, boilerplate, admin/contact, headings, datelines) — table predicates were removed from the boundary after the blast showed figure-dense continuations (`million and $687 million in pre-tax gains ...`) scoring as table lines and stopping the block at the key line. The headline loop takes `include_paren=False`: a paren line stays headline-eligible there (batch 126 — `(B)Crude oil ...` headlines a supplement packet that must return None; `(2026) ACME CORP ...` headlines are preserved). The lede also drops investor-conference-call notices (`conference call` + `webcast`/`replay`, singular `call` with a word boundary — AppLovin's `Webcast and Conference Calls` heading is plural and is not a notice). **Blast-radius story:** the first blast showed 12 diffs — 6 EOG (the headline loop was skipping paren lines, defeating the batch-126 supplement guard) and 6 COIN/EMR; the `include_paren` fix cleared all 6 EOG diffs. The second blast showed the COIN footnote tails still served (the boundary bug + the completeness gate) and the EMR call-notice backfill; the boundary/completeness fixes plus the call-notice exclusion and the `US`-lead/`excludes?` refinements left exactly 7 diffs, all intended: EMR ×4 (footnote tails only) and COIN ×3 (footnote text out; the rounding paragraph now fills the lede). A third blast confirmed the APP false positive was gone (`Webcast and Conference Calls` heading no longer matches the notice regex). **Live repair: 4 stored rows replaced** under the batch-157 gates (re-fetched inside the repair script; live source label equals stored; live ≥200 chars; stored headline kept verbatim; every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence survives; live carries no table residue and no call notice; repair live text byte-identical to the blast's new extraction for all 4): EMR 2024-12-31 (1816 → 1710, `1 Results are presented...` + `2 Underlying orders...` out), EMR 2025-03-31 (2382 → 2339, `1 Results are presented...` out), EMR 2025-12-31 (1535 → 1377, both `1`/`2` Underlying lines out), EMR 2026-03-31 (1449 → 1359, both lines out). The probe/blast/repair scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch166_probe.py`, `batch166_blast.py`, `batch166_repair.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent; the blast HTML cache lives under the goal's `hidden_files/blast166_html` (`/tmp` proved unreliable across exec calls).

**Tests:** 15 new in `tests/data/test_filings_batch166.py` (verbatim COIN Q4 FY2025 block consumes 17 lines; complete-looking key line still wraps — the [3] $USD chain consumes 13; bare-term opener predicate incl. figure-line negatives; opener predicates incl. the term+verb variant, mixed-case terms, and the `is expected` / `are included` / digit-in-term negatives; shape-1 wrapped definition consumes 6; bracketed block consumes 5; `$`-led continuation consumes; year-led sentence does not continue; GEV single-line legend never eats the headline; `US`-lead continuation consumes 7; fourth shape drops from the lede under a comma tail; bracketed footnotes drop from the lede; call notice drops from the lede; paren footnote stays headline-eligible). Full suite 1217 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 268 quarters across 34 filers (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV; HTML cached so both runs see byte-identical input) shows 7 diffs, all intended: EMR ×4 (footnote tails only, guidance prose kept) and COIN ×3 (footnote text out). The other 261 quarters are byte-identical, including all GEV quarters, all shareholder-letter, image-only, and Tesla quarters, and SHOP Q3 2024. **Blast-radius method note:** the first blast (12 diffs) is what produced the `include_paren` headline-loop guard; the second (12 diffs, different set) is what produced the boundary/completeness fixes, the call-notice exclusion, and the `US`-lead/`excludes?` refinements; the third confirmed the APP heading false positive was gone. ### Batch 167 — Rounding-note + asterisk-note skips; quarter titles are headlines; 3 stale COIN rows repaired (2026-11-01)
Closes the batch-166 COIN follow-ups in full. Coinbase's letters carry a rounding boilerplate paragraph ahead of the letter prose — the long form ("Figures have been rounded for presentation purposes only. For additional financial information and a reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP results, please refer to the reconciliation of GAAP to Non-GAAP results table(s) in this shareholder letter and our Form 10-K/10-Q filed with the SEC on <date>.") and the short form ("Note: Figures presented [above] may not sum precisely due to rounding."). **fix** (`data/filings.py`): `_rounding_note_block_len` skips the note as a block at all four collection paths (headline loop, lede, leading-bullet block, Highlights sections) plus a sentence-level check in the salvage path. Consumption runs to sentence termination taking any non-boundary line as a continuation — unlike the footnote block, a note's wraps are ordinary sentence fragments ("GAAP results table in this shareholder letter and our", "Coinbase's Class A common stock", "Form 10-Q filed with the SEC on May 8, 2025."), so the footnote block's plausibility test would stop the block mid-note and serve the rest; the opener is the guard instead (both regexes match boilerplate only). After termination the block chains only onto "For additional financial information" (the long form's second sentence, for when the sentence break falls on a line boundary); a following note is its own opener. The same batch adds the orphaned-sentence form as an opener: Q3 FY2024 carries the short note plus the referral sentence with no long-form first sentence, so the referral paragraph was served as lede content. **fix:** `_asterisk_note_block_len` skips chart asterisk notes ("* Based on the closing price of Coinbase's Class A common stock on May 7, 2025 on the Nasdaq Global Stock Market.", "*Below reporting threshold of 10%.", "*Net of USDC loaned or pledged as collateral.", "*As of December 31, 2024, USDC excludes ...", "* Measures used in this release ...") as blocks at the same four paths. The lede's table-line rule already dropped the opener, but its wrapped continuations were served — "Coinbase's Class A common stock" opened Q1 FY2025's lede once the rounding note was skipped — and in the leading-bullet block the opener starts a bullet item. The lead list is deliberately note-shaped (Based on / As of / Net of / Below / Measures used): a highlights bullet never opens with one, and the batch-165 "* Revenue grew 12 percent." bullet stays a bullet. **fix:** quarter titles are headlines, not masthead material — "First Quarter 2025" / "Second Quarter 2025" were skipped twice over: `_looks_like_masthead_residue` swallowed the 3-word form as a street / city-postal line (≤4 words + digit) and the headline loop's masthead branch skipped it when the date line followed, and `_skip_bare_masthead_name` read it as a company name (the date + page number count as residue and the first footnote line — "[1] Adjusted EBITDA is a non-GAAP financial", unterminated, so headline-shaped — counts as the real headline). Q1 survived by luck: its "[1] This is a non-GAAP financial measure." ends with a period, so the bare-masthead lookahead returned None. Both skips now exclude quarter titles (`_QUARTER_TITLE_RE`: "First/Second/Third/Fourth Quarter [and Full Year] YYYY"), so the title is picked as the headline — the same shape Q4's "Fourth Quarter and Full Year 2024" already had. The design considered a fallback ("use the skipped title when no other headline is found") and rejected it: with the title skipped, the loop would pick the letter's first prose sentence as a Franken-headline ("We delivered strong financial results in Q1, ... Fellow Shareholders,") via the wrap joiner, so the fallback would have to fire on prose-shaped picks — stopping the skip is cleaner and the blast proves no title steals a real verb headline in the cohort. **Live repair: 3 stored rows replaced** under the batch-157 gates with the batch-164 defect-body branch (the stored body IS the defect — rounding note + footnote text — so no stored bullet core or sentence is required to survive; the gates are headline kept verbatim + the live body opens with the letter prose + no rounding/footnote/asterisk residue): COIN 2024-12-31 (322 → 776, rounding paragraph → letter prose), COIN 2025-03-31 (457 → 1119, metric bullet + MTU footnote → letter prose), COIN 2025-06-30 (246 → 678, "[1]/[2]" footnote text → letter prose). The repair's live text is byte-identical to the blast's new extraction for all 3. Q1 FY2025's stored MTU bullet ("Since implementing the current version of our rewards program in June 2023, the number of MTUs") is an accepted non-survival: a metric-area bullet whose continuation is the MTU footnote definition; the letter path serves the opening prose, not the metric table. The probe/blast/pin/repair scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch167_probe.py`, `batch167_blast.py`, `batch167_pin.py`, `batch167_repair.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent.

**Tests:** 17 new in `tests/data/test_filings_batch167.py` (rounding-note regex positives incl. the orphaned referral sentence + negatives; long form consumes 6 lines; short wrapped form consumes 2; chain covers the sentence break on a line boundary; orphaned referral sentence consumes 8; asterisk-note regex positives incl. all five leads spaced + attached + negatives incl. the "* Revenue grew" bullet, the bare glyph, the attached-glyph footnote definition, and the deliberately uncovered "During" / quoted-term forms; asterisk block consumes 4 lines; single-line forms consume 1; quarter-title regex positives + negatives; quarter title is not masthead residue (address lines still are); quarter title is not a bare masthead name (verbatim Q2 2025 head); COIN Q1/Q2/Q4 end-to-end with verbatim heads; orphaned-referral end-to-end; "*" bullets still serve; salvage drops rounding-note sentences). The regression is pinned behaviorally (`scripts/batch167_pin.py`, old-code-only imports): 8 of 10 checks fail on stashed old code, 0 on new. Full suite 1234 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 268 quarters across 34 filers (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV; HTML cached so both runs see byte-identical input) shows 4 diffs, all intended: COIN Q4 FY2024 (rounding paragraph → letter prose), Q1 FY2025 (rounding-note headline → "First Quarter 2025" + letter prose, asterisk tail out), Q2 FY2025 (same), Q3 FY2025 (headline becomes "Third Quarter 2025" — matching the stored headline — and the lede gains its last prose line, "trading, and the close of the Deribit acquisition."). The other 264 quarters are byte-identical, including all GEV quarters (footnote guard untouched), all shareholder-letter, image-only, and Tesla quarters, and SHOP Q3 2024 (salvage intact). The orphaned-sentence opener changes nothing in the cohort (new-vs-new2 blast is empty) — it only fires on Q3 FY2024, which is not in the cache. **Blast-radius method note:** the first probe showed Q1 fixed but Q2 still serving prose as its headline — the trace (settled by `sys.settrace` on the headline loop) showed `_skip_bare_masthead_name` jumping i=4→7, which is what produced the second guard; the bare-masthead rule's lookahead reads the unterminated "[1] Adjusted EBITDA is a non-GAAP financial" as headline-shaped. ### Batch 168 — Footnote second sentences + paren wrap tails; COIN Q3 FY2024 repaired (2026-11-01)
Closes the batch-167 COIN Q3 FY2024 follow-up in full. The stored Q3 FY2024 row was the title + rounding note + the [1] footnote itself, and its live text served two footnote residues. **fix 1** (`data/filings.py`): `_footnote_def_block_len` gains an anaphoric second-sentence chain — after a definition terminates, the next line is consumed as part of the block when it opens with an anaphoric determiner (These/This/Those/That/Both/Such) whose noun appears in the definition just consumed ("These losses were $92 million / after reflecting the tax impact." — "losses" is in the [1] definition's first sentence). A capital-led sentence after a terminated definition is otherwise indistinguishable from new prose, so the noun test is the guard; it is exact, not stemmed, and the definition's words accumulate across its wrapped lines (the noun sits on a wrap line, not the key line). The second sentence's own wraps consume by the normal plausibility rules, and the chain then resumes onto the next definition opener, so the whole [1]/[2]/[3] legend drops as one 12-line block. (Q4 FY2024's identical shape escapes only because its second sentence starts mid-line, "These gains were" sharing the termination line.) **fix 2:** `_is_plausible_continuation` gains a parenthetical abbreviation branch — a line starting with 2+ all-caps letters opening a lowercase parenthetical ("USDC (net of USDC lent or", the [3] definition's wrap tail) is a plausible wrap. The 4-cap lead failed the 2–3-cap abbreviation branch; unlike that branch the termination guard is dropped, because a tail that terminates on its own line ("USDC (net of USDC lent or pledged as collateral).") is still the same definition's tail. The no-digits guard keeps figure-led prose out, and the line is only ever reached as the wrap of an unterminated definition. **Live repair: 1 stored row replaced** under the batch-157 gates with the batch-164 defect-body branch: COIN 2024-09-30 (210 → 493, title + rounding note + [1] footnote → letter prose under the "Third Quarter 2024" headline). The repair's live text is byte-identical to the blast's new extraction. The probe/blast/repair scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch168_probe.py`, `batch168_blast.py`, `batch168_repair.py`) per the batch-105–110 precedent; the Q3 FY2024 exhibit HTML is cached under the goal's `hidden_files/blast168_html/`.

**Tests:** 4 new in `tests/data/test_filings_batch168.py` (verbatim Q3 FY2024 footnote block consumes 12 lines; anaphora needs the noun in the definition — "These results" without "results" stops the block, the "Both measurements" plural mismatch stops it, a fresh-subject sentence stops it; paren-tail positives incl. the terminated single-line form + digit-carrying and capital-paren negatives; Q3 FY2024 end-to-end with the verbatim head). The regression is pinned by stashing the src change: 3 of the 4 new tests fail without it (the negative-guard test passes on old code too, by design — it pins the guard, not the fix). Full suite 1238 green, `git diff --check` clean. **Blast-radius:** old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 269 quarters across 34 filers (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV + the new COIN Q3 FY2024 exhibit; HTML cached so both runs see byte-identical input) shows 1 diff, intended: COIN Q3 FY2024 (the two footnote residues out, lede opens with the letter prose). The other 268 quarters are byte-identical, including all GEV quarters (footnote guard untouched), all shareholder-letter, image-only, and Tesla quarters, and SHOP Q3 2024 (salvage intact). **Carried follow-ups:** Still open: ABNB Q3/Q4 2024's prose-interleaved tables (deliberately unparsed); CART "And …" sentence (genuine body text — no repair applies); ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence (stored bodies preserved by splice; the body drift itself is a separate extractor question); AFRM Q4 FY2026 + LIN Q3 2024 image-only blanks (OCR-only) alongside EOG 8/8 supplement-only blanks and SKYD 4 pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 still NULL (latest deck, unprobed — likely unfilled rather than a defect); pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation; the footnote-tail rule still stops at 6 words (CCI's long labels never reach extracted output); STZ live quote paragraphs interleave two-column quote text (real content, flattening artifact — the bullets are clean). **From batch 157's gates (6 skips, each a real content question):** ADBE Q4 FY2025 (stored FY totals + Disclosure Updates paragraph lost); ADBE Q2 FY2026 (Semrush targets sentence lost); META Q3 2025 ($15.93B tax-charge paragraph lost); APP Q3 2024 (dateline paragraph with the shareholder-letter mention lost); MCO Q3 2026 (CEO quote + attribution lost); EMR Q3 2025 (Outlook guidance paragraph lost); V Q4 FY2025 lede-vs-section divergence; V Q2 FY2026 has no stored highlights row at all. **New from this batch:** the anaphora noun test is exact, so the Q4 FY2025 "Both measurements represent ..." second sentence stays uncovered (its definition says "period of measurement") — a plural-tolerant test is its own design, and it must handle the wrapped-term-key interaction (consuming the second sentence would then consume the following "4 Coinbase Crypto Trading Volume" key line via the digit-led plausibility rule).
